I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, and I had a dance class, and I’d walk with some of the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t make it to class or the walks much. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F33.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I got scared by some thoughts I’ve been having. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I haven’t tried to hurt myself or anything like that. But I keep thinking maybe it would be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. That didn’t seem like something I should keep to myself.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about four months now. Around when my aunt came to live with us. It wasn’t constant at first, or at least I tried not to make too much of it, but that’s about when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still like those things, I think. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, and I had a dance class, and I’d walk with some of the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t make it to class or the walks much. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t know if I’d call it restless. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I do feel slower. Even at the market, I’ll stand there too long before picking up what I came for, like I have to work myself up to it. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just takes me longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’d call significant. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been watching the scale closely. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it’s even started. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Those were nice things for me.\n\nLately, though, I just leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t go like I should. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t mean I never do anything at all, but most of the time I just don’t feel drawn to the things I used to enjoy. My watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies… I used to look forward to those. Lately I just leave my things sitting there and can’t seem to make myself care. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That didn’t seem like something I should keep to myself.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about four months now. Around when my aunt came to live with us. It wasn’t constant at first, or at least I tried not to make too much of it, but that’s about when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still like those things, I think. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, and I had a dance class, and I’d walk with some of the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t make it to class or the walks much. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t know if I’d call it restless. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I do feel slower. Even at the market, I’ll stand there too long before picking up what I came for, like I have to work myself up to it. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just takes me longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’d call significant. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been watching the scale closely. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it’s even started. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Those were nice things for me.\n\nLately, though, I just leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t go like I should. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t mean I never do anything at all, but most of the time I just don’t feel drawn to the things I used to enjoy. My watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies… I used to look forward to those. Lately I just leave my things sitting there and can’t seem to make myself care. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to look forward to my watercolors, and going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my things sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this consistent sadness?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with us. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty steady since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t know if I’d call it restless. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I do feel slower. Even at the market, I’ll stand there too long before picking up what I came for, like I have to work myself up to it. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just takes me longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’d call significant. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been watching the scale closely. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it’s even started. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Those were nice things for me.\n\nLately, though, I just leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t go like I should. I can’t seem to make myself care about it the way I used to. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t mean I never do anything at all, but most of the time I just don’t feel drawn to the things I used to enjoy. My watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies… I used to look forward to those. Lately I just leave my things sitting there and can’t seem to make myself care. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to look forward to my watercolors, and going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my things sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this consistent sadness?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with us. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty steady since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still try sometimes, so I don’t want to sound like I’ve given up completely. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t feel that pull anymore. Mostly I leave my things sitting there and can’t make myself care. Is that an okay way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this kind of sadness before, prior to the past four months?\n\nPatient: No… not like this, I don’t think. I’ve been sad before, of course, but before my aunt moved in and all this heaviness started, I never had things like… hearing my mother’s voice, or feeling like she was watching me. That only seems to happen on the really bad days, when I feel like I’ve failed everyone. I’m sorry, is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. Lately I just sort of leave my things sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this consistent sadness?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with us. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty steady since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still try sometimes, so I don’t want to sound like I’ve given up completely. I’ve been sad before, of course, but before my aunt moved in and all this heaviness started, I never had things like… hearing my mother’s voice, or feeling like she was watching me. That only seems to happen on the really bad days, when I feel like I’ve failed everyone. I mean, I’ve had sadness in my life, but I never heard my mother’s voice or felt like she was watching me before I sank into this after my aunt moved in. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t feel that pull anymore. Mostly I leave my things sitting there and can’t make myself care. Is that an okay way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this kind of sadness before, prior to the past four months?\n\nPatient: No… not like this, I don’t think. I’ve been sad before, of course, but before my aunt moved in and all this heaviness started, I never had things like… hearing my mother’s voice, or feeling like she was watching me. That only seems to happen on the really bad days, when I feel like I’ve failed everyone. I’m sorry, is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you had an episode like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not like this before. I mean, I’ve had sadness in my life, but I never heard my mother’s voice or felt like she was watching me before I sank into this after my aunt moved in. It’s mostly only on the really bad, heavy days, when I feel like I’ve failed everyone… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of persistent sadness like this before the past four months?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had it like this before. I mean, I’ve had bad days, but lately most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it even starts. That’s… that’s new for me, I think. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I got scared by some thoughts I’ve been having. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I haven’t tried to hurt myself or anything like that. But I keep thinking maybe it would be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. That didn’t seem like something I should keep to myself.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about four months now. Around when my aunt came to live with us. It wasn’t constant at first, or at least I tried not to make too much of it, but that’s about when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still like those things, I think. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, and I had a dance class, and I’d walk with some of the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t make it to class or the walks much. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t know if I’d call it restless. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I do feel slower. Even at the market, I’ll stand there too long before picking up what I came for, like I have to work myself up to it. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just takes me longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’d call significant. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been watching the scale closely. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it’s even started. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I used to really look forward to doing my watercolors, and I had a dance class, and I’d walk with some of the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my paints sitting there, and I don’t make it to class or the walks much. I can’t seem to make myself care the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you do when you actually make yourself do them, like painting or dancing?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean… if I make myself sit down with the watercolors or go to dance, sometimes it’s okay, but I don’t really look forward to it anymore. Mostly the paints just sit there, and I don’t seem to care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic… it’s just different than before.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you still enjoy doing, or has the pleasure gone out of almost everything for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But the things I used to look forward to—my watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies—I just don’t seem to reach for them anymore. I’ll leave the paints sitting there, or I’ll think about going and then not go. It’s like I can’t make myself care the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose once in a while something feels nice, but I don’t really look forward to things like I used to. My watercolors are just sitting there, and I keep missing dance class and the walks with the ladies. I don’t mean to make too much of it, but it’s like I can’t quite make myself care enough to do them. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, more than I probably should. I try not to dwell on it, but when I get tired or irritated with my aunt, I start thinking, “What kind of woman am I?” Like I’m being ungrateful, or bringing shame on my family for even feeling that way. I know that may sound silly… but it does get to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t know if I’d call it restless. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I do feel slower. Even at the market, I’ll stand there too long before picking up what I came for, like I have to work myself up to it. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything… it just takes me longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’d call significant. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been watching the scale closely. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness in my chest, like I’ve already failed the day before it’s even started. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that still bring you a sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m being honest… I mean, I still try to act like I’m interested. Is that… is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. My watercolors, dance class, walking with the ladies… I used to look forward to those. Lately I just leave my things sitting there and can’t seem to make myself care. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I used to look forward to my watercolors, and going to dance class, and walking with the ladies. Lately I just sort of leave my things sitting there, and I can’t seem to make myself care like I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this consistent sadness?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months now. It started around when my aunt came to live with us. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty steady since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still try sometimes, so I don’t want to sound like I’ve given up completely.
